وَالْقَوْلُ بِمِثْلِ هَذَا كَبِيرَةٌ وَمَصِيرٌ إِلَى التَّحَكُّمِ فِي الشَّرْعِ بِالْأَهْوَاءِ وَالْإِرَادَاتِ، نَعُوذُ بِاللَّهِ مِنْ ذَلِكَ. بَلِ الْأَدِلَّةُ الشَّرْعِيَّةُ، كَمَا تَقَدَّمَ مِنْ قَوْلِنَا، إِمَّا قَطْعِيَّةٌ، وَإِمَّا ظَنِّيَّةٌ أَكْثَرِيَّةٌ وَجَبَ الْعَمَلُ بِهَا بِالْأَصْلِ الْمَقْطُوعِ بِهِ. وَمَا كَانَ هَكَذَا فَالْحُكْمُ لَا شَكَّ يَحْصُلُ عَنْهَا بِذَاتِهَا، لَا بِحَسَبِ مَا طُبِعَ عَلَيْهِ إِنْسَانٌ إِنْسَانٌ، بَلْ بِحَسَبِ دَلَالَتِهَا،

To say such a thing is a grave offence and a resort to arbitrary judgement in the Law through desires and inclinations; we seek God’s protection from it. Legal evidence, as we said, is either decisive or preponderantly probable, with action on the latter required by a certainly established principle. In either case the ruling undoubtedly follows from the evidence itself, according to its signification, not according to each person’s natural disposition.[N115: translation] The author’s sharp criticism is directed at the account of evidence just reported from Abū Ḥāmid. The editor’s separate comparison with the more flexible treatment in Bidāyat al-mujtahid is not merged into Ibn Rushd’s authorial voice. ↩[N116: textual] Printed الطلب is understood here as the result sought in inquiry, in light of المطلوب in the following sentence. ↩

وَلِذَلِكَ مَا كَانَ مِنَ الْأَدِلَّةِ مُتَرَدِّدًا بَيْنَ النَّقِيضَيْنِ عَلَى السَّوَاءِ سُمِّيَ فِي هَذِهِ الصِّنَاعَةِ مُجْمَلًا، وَلَمْ يُجْعَلْ دَلِيلًا شَرْعِيًّا أَصْلًا. وَبِالْجُمْلَةِ الْقَوْلُ بِأَنْ لَيْسَ هُنَا أَدِلَّةٌ يَحْصُلُ عَنْهَا بِذَاتِهَا الطَّلَبُ، سَوَاءٌ كَانَتِ الْأَدِلَّةُ عَقْلِيَّةً أَوْ شَرْعِيَّةً، هُوَ قَوْلٌ سُوفَسْطَائِيٌّ جِدًّا، وَيَنْبَغِي أَنْ يُتَجَنَّبَ، فَإِنَّهُ عَظِيمُ الضَّرَرِ فِي الشَّرِيعَةِ. وَإِذَا كَانَ هَذَا هَكَذَا، وَكَانَ هَهُنَا أَدِلَّةٌ تُفْضِي إِلَى الْمَطْلُوبِ، لَمْ يَكُنْ تَكْلِيفُ إِصَابَتِهِ مِنْ بَابِ مَا لَا يُطَاقُ.

Hence an indication undecided equally between contradictories is called indeterminate in this discipline and is not accepted as legal evidence at all. In general, denying that evidence itself yields what is sought—whether the evidence is rational or legal—is a thoroughly sophistical claim, to be avoided because of its great harm to the Law. Since evidence leading to the object sought exists, requiring one to attain it is not an obligation beyond one’s capacity.

§242 · Al-Ghazālī’s second argument: absence of sin

وَأَمَّا الطَّرِيقَةُ الْأُخْرَى الَّتِي احْتَجَّ بِهَا فَأَخَذَهَا مِنَ الْوَاجِبِ، وَذَلِكَ أَنَّهُ كَمَا قِيلَ فِيهِ هُوَ مَا فِي تَرْكِهِ عِقَابٌ قَالَ: فَلَوْ كَانَ الْوَاجِبُ فِي الشَّيْءِ حُكْمًا مُتَعَيِّنًا لَكَانَ فِي تَرْكِ إِصَابَتِهِ إِثْمٌ، وَالْإِجْمَاعُ قَدِ انْعَقَدَ عَلَى سُقُوطِ التَّأْثِيمِ عَنِ الْمُجْتَهِدِينَ، فَلِذَلِكَ يَلْزَمُ عَنِ انْتِفَاءِ التَّأْثِيمِ انْتِفَاءُ الْخَطَأِ.

His other argument is drawn from obligation. Since the obligatory was defined as that whose omission incurs punishment, he argues: if the obligation concerning a thing were a determinate ruling, failure to attain it would incur sin. But consensus establishes that independent jurists are not charged with sin. Therefore, the absence of sin entails the absence of error.

§243 · An error can be pardoned without becoming correct

وَنَحْنُ نَقُولُ إِنَّهُ لَيْسَ يَلْزَمُ عَنْ سُقُوطِ التَّأْثِيمِ انْتِفَاءُ الْخَطَأِ، إِذِ الْخَطَأُ غَيْرُ الْمُتَعَمَّدِ مَصْفُوحٌ عَنْهُ فِي الشَّرْعِ، وَهُوَ يَقَعُ بِهِ النِّسْيَانُ وَالْغَفْلَةُ وَالسَّهْوُ. وَقَدْ قُلْنَا غَيْرَ مَا مَرَّةٍ إِنَّ الْأَدِلَّةَ الشَّرْعِيَّةَ إِمَّا قَطْعِيَّةٌ، وَإِمَّا أَكْثَرِيَّةٌ. أَمَّا الْقَطْعِيَّةُ فَالطَّرِيقُ إِلَيْهَا مَهْيَعٌ، وَوُقُوعُ السَّهْوِ فِيهَا وَالْغَلَطِ يَقِلُّ، فَلِذَلِكَ يُشْبِهُ أَنْ يَلْزَمَ التَّأْثِيمُ فِيهَا بِلُزُومِ الْخَطَأِ. وَأَمَّا الظَّنِّيَّةُ فَالْغَلَطُ كَثِيرًا مَا يَقَعُ فِيهَا وَالسَّهْوُ، فَلِذَلِكَ لَمْ يَلْزَمْ مِنَ الْخَطَأِ فِيهَا التَّأْثِيمُ.

We say that exemption from sin does not entail absence of error. Unintentional error is pardoned in the Law; it occurs through forgetfulness, inattention, and inadvertence. We have repeatedly said that legal evidence is either decisive or preponderant. The path to decisive evidence is plainly accessible, and inadvertence and mistake are rare in it; hence error there seems to entail sin. With probable evidence, however, mistake and inadvertence occur often, so error does not entail sin.[N117: textual] The compressed clause وهو يقع به النسيان والغفلة والسهو has an awkward pronoun/preposition sequence. Its contextual point is that forgetfulness and inadvertence belong to the pardoned class of unintentional error; the English expresses this relation without claiming a new Arabic reading. ↩

وَالَّذِي أُخِذَ فِي حَدِّ الْوَاجِبِ بِأَنَّهُ الَّذِي فِي تَرْكِهِ عِقَابٌ، إِنَّمَا مَعْنَى ذَلِكَ أَنَّ الْعِقَابَ يَتَعَلَّقُ بِتَرْكِهِ بَعْدَ الْمَعْرِفَةِ بِوُجُوبِهِ، فِيمَا كَانَ إِلَى الْمَعْرِفَةِ بِوُجُوبِهِ دَلِيلٌ قَطْعِيٌّ. وَأَمَّا مَنْ غَلِطَ أَوْ سَهَا فِيمَا الْغَلَطُ فِيهِ مُعَرَّضٌ فَلَمْ يَكُنْ لَهُ سَبِيلٌ إِلَى مَعْرِفَةِ وُجُوبِهِ، فَكَيْفَ يَلْحَقُ بِمِثْلِ هَذَا تَأْثِيمٌ. وَقَدْ قَالَ عَلَيْهِ السَّلَامُ: «رُفِعَ عَنْ أُمَّتِيَ الْخَطَأُ وَالنِّسْيَانُ».

The definition of the obligatory as that whose omission incurs punishment means that punishment attaches to omission after its obligatory status has become known, where decisive evidence leads to that knowledge. If someone errs or is inadvertent in a matter liable to error and thus has no way to know its obligatory status, how could he incur sin? The Prophet, peace be upon him, has said, “Error and forgetfulness have been lifted from my community.”

§244 · Degrees of culpability and the clarity of evidence

فَإِنْ قِيلَ فَسَيَلْزَمُ عَلَى هَذَا أَنَّ مَا كَانَ عَلَيْهِ دَلِيلٌ قَطْعِيٌّ فَأَخْطَأَهُ الْمُجْتَهِدُ عَلَى غَيْرِ عَمْدٍ أَنَّهُ لَا يَأْثَمُ. قِيلَ فِي هَذَا نَظَرٌ. وَالْفَرْقُ بَيْنَهُمَا بَيِّنٌ. فَإِنَّ الدَّلِيلَ الْقَطْعِيَّ قَلَّمَا يَخْفَى عَلَى أَحَدٍ مِمَّنْ بَلَغَ رُتْبَةَ الِاجْتِهَادِ، وَإِنَّمَا يُتَصَوَّرُ خَفَاؤُهُ لِهَوًى أَوْ هَوَادَةٍ أَوْ لِشَيْءٍ عَلَى خِلَافِهِ أَوْ غَيْرِ ذَلِكَ مِنَ الْعَوَارِضِ النَّفْسِيَّةِ. وَلِهَذَا مَرَاتِبُ بِحَسَبِ مَرَاتِبِ الْأَدِلَّةِ، وَلِذَلِكَ يُكَفَّرُ فِي بَعْضِهَا، وَيُؤَثَّمُ فِي بَعْضٍ، وَمُدْرَكُ هَذَا التَّفَاوُتِ الشَّرْعُ.

If it is said that this entails freedom from sin even when a jurist unintentionally misses something established by decisive evidence, we reply: this requires examination. The difference is clear. Decisive evidence is seldom hidden from someone who has attained the rank of independent inquiry. Its being hidden is conceivable only through desire, partiality, something opposing it, or other psychological impediments. This has degrees corresponding to the degrees of evidence. Hence some cases incur a judgement of unbelief and others a charge of sin; the Law determines these differences.

§245 · Attainable truth and merciful pardon

فَقَدْ تَبَيَّنَ مِنْ قَوْلِنَا أَنْ لَيْسَ كُلُّ مُجْتَهِدٍ مُصِيبًا، وَإِنَّهُ إِنْ أَخْطَأَ فَعَلَى أَيِّ جِهَةٍ لَا يَأْثَمُ، وَإِنَّ الْمُجْتَهِدَ كُلِّفَ إِصَابَةَ مَا هُوَ فِي نَفْسِهِ مُمْكِنُ الْإِصَابَةِ، وَعُفِيَ عَنْهُ عِنْدَ الْخَطَأِ رَحْمَةً لَهُ وَصَفْحًا عَنْهُ.

Our discussion has shown that not every independent jurist is correct, in what respect he is free from sin when he errs, and that he is charged with attaining something that is itself attainable. When he errs, he is forgiven out of mercy and pardon.

§246 · Conflict: suspension, caution or free choice?

وَقَدْ بَقِيَ عَلَيْنَا مِنْ هَذَا الْبَابِ كَيْفَ حَالَةُ الْمُجْتَهِدِ، إِذَا تَعَارَضَتْ عِنْدَهُ الْأَدِلَّةُ فِي مَسْأَلَةٍ مَا، وَإِلَى أَيِّ شَيْءٍ يَصِيرُ فَنَقُولُ: إِنَّ الَّذِي حَكَى فِي ذَلِكَ أَبُو حَامِدٍ ثَلَاثَةُ آرَاءٍ: أَحَدُهَا رَأْيُ مَنْ يَرَى التَّوَقُّفَ وَالثَّانِي الْأَخْذُ بِالْأَحْوَطِ وَالثَّالِثُ رَأْيُ الْقَاضِي وَهُوَ أَنْ يَتَخَيَّرَ الْمُجْتَهِدُ، وَهَذَا رَأْيٌ ضَعِيفٌ لِأَنَّ التَّخْيِيرَ إِبَاحَةٌ، وَالْأَدِلَّةُ الْمُتَنَاقِضَةُ فِي الْأَمْرِ بِالشَّيْءِ الْوَاحِدِ هِيَ أَوْلَى أَنْ تُوقِعَ الشَّكَّ وَالْحَيْرَةَ مِنْ أَنْ يُظَنَّ بِهَا أَنَّهَا تُنْتِجُهُ.

It remains to consider the jurist’s position when evidence conflicts for him on a question, and what course he should take. Abū Ḥāmid reports three views: suspending judgement, adopting the more cautious course, and the Judge’s view that the jurist may choose. This last view is weak, because granting a choice is permission, whereas contradictory evidence concerning the command about a single matter is more apt to produce doubt and perplexity than to be thought to establish such permission.[N118: translation] The compressed bathing examples concern whether emission is required or whether intercourse itself requires the ritual bath. The Arabic formulations are retained. The “Judge” is left as the author’s designation; no unsupported personal identification is inserted. ↩

وَكَذَلِكَ التَّوَقُّفُ لَا مَعْنَى لَهُ، فَإِنَّ فِي ذَلِكَ تَعْطِيلًا لِلْأَحْكَامِ. وَأَيْضًا فَإِنَّهُ غَيْرُ مُمْكِنٍ فِي الْأَشْيَاءِ الَّتِي لَيْسَ يُمْكِنُ الْإِنْسَانَ فِيهَا إِلَّا أَنْ يَأْتِيَ أَحَدَ الضِّدَّيْنِ، كَالْأَحَادِيثِ الْوَارِدَةِ مَثَلًا فِي الْغُسْلِ مِنَ الْمَاءِ، وَالْغُسْلِ مِنِ الْتِقَاءِ الْخِتَانَيْنِ، فَإِنَّ مِثْلَ هَذِهِ الْأَحَادِيثِ إِذَا تَعَارَضَتْ لَمْ يُمْكِنْ فِيهَا التَّوَقُّفُ، فَإِنَّهُ لَا بُدَّ مِنَ الْمَصِيرِ إِلَى أَحَدِهِمَا.

Suspending judgement likewise makes no sense, since it leaves rulings inoperative. It is also impossible where a person must do one of two opposite things. Consider the reports about ritual bathing because of the emission of fluid, and bathing because of the meeting of the circumcised parts. When such reports conflict, suspension is impossible: one must adopt one or the other.

وَفِي مِثْلِ هَذَا يُخَيَّلُ الْمَصِيرُ إِلَى الْأَخْذِ بِالْأَحْوَطِ. وَهُوَ وَإِنْ كَانَ يُخَيَّلُ فِيهِ أَنَّهُ أَوْلَى لِمَكَانِ النَّجَاةِ مِنَ الذَّمِّ، فَكَذَلِكَ يُخَافُ لُحُوقُ الذَّمِّ بِزِيَادَةِ مَا لَيْسَ مِنَ الشَّرْعِ فِي الشَّرْعِ.

In such a case, adopting the more cautious course appears plausible. Although it may seem preferable because it avoids blame, one must equally fear blame for adding to the Law something that does not belong to it.

§247 · The Ẓāhirī response: return to original nonliability

وَلِلظَّاهِرِيَّةِ فِي هَذَا قَوْلٌ رَابِعٌ، وَهُوَ أَنْ يَرْجِعَ الْمُجْتَهِدُ عِنْدَ تَكَافُؤِ الْأَدِلَّةِ إِلَى الْبَرَاءَةِ الْأَصْلِيَّةِ، لِأَنَّ التَّكْلِيفَ بِالْأَدِلَّةِ الْمُتَنَاقِضَةِ تَكْلِيفُ مَا لَا يُطَاقُ، وَهُوَ فِي ذَلِكَ بِمَنْزِلَةِ التَّكْلِيفِ بِمَا لَيْسَ عَلَيْهِ دَلِيلٌ. فَكَمَا أَنَّ مَا لَيْسَ عَلَيْهِ دَلِيلٌ تُسْتَصْحَبُ فِيهِ الْبَرَاءَةُ الْأَصْلِيَّةُ، أَوْ مَا كَانَ عَلَيْهِ دَلِيلٌ إِلَّا أَنَّهُ لَمْ يُمْكِنْ بُلُوغُهُ، كَذَلِكَ مَنْ تَعَارَضَتْ عِنْدَهُ الْأَدِلَّةُ فِي شَيْءٍ مَا سَاقِطَةٌ فِي حَقِّهِ.

The Ẓāhirīs have a fourth view: when the evidence is equally balanced, the independent jurist returns to original freedom from obligation. An obligation imposed through contradictory evidence exceeds one’s capacity and is like an obligation lacking evidence. Just as original freedom from obligation is presumed to continue where there is no evidence, or where evidence exists but cannot be reached, so conflicting evidence concerning something falls away for the person confronted with it.[N119: translation] The opening explicitly reports a Ẓāhirī position. The final sentence distinguishes conflict as it appears to particular inquirers from contradiction in the Law itself; the translation does not erase that distinction. ↩

وَإِنَّمَا يَقَعُ هَذَانِ الصِّنْفَانِ فِي حَقِّ قَوْمٍ وَأَهْلِ زَمَانٍ مَا مِمَّنْ لَمْ يَصِلْهُمُ الدَّلِيلُ الشَّرْعِيُّ، أَوْ مِمَّنْ لَمْ يَتَمَيَّزْ لَهُمُ النَّاسِخُ فِي ذَلِكَ وَالْمَنْسُوخُ وَغَيْرُ ذَلِكَ مِمَّا يُوجِبُ التَّعَارُضَ، إِذْ كَانَ لَيْسَ يَجُوزُ وُقُوعُ دَلَائِلَ مُتَضَادَّةٍ فِي الشَّرْعِ.

These two conditions occur only relative to particular people or people of a particular period: those whom the legal evidence has not reached, or who have not distinguished the abrogating from the abrogated, or other matters producing conflict. For contradictory evidence cannot occur within the Law itself.

§248 · From independent inquiry to following authority

وَإِذْ قَدْ فَرَغْنَا مِنَ الْقَوْلِ فِي الِاجْتِهَادِ وَالْمُجْتَهَدِ فِيهِ، فَلْنَقُلْ فِي التَّقْلِيدِ وَالْمُقَلِّدِ.

Having completed the discussion of independent inquiry and its subject matter, let us discuss following authority and the person who does so.

§249 · Trust, probability and following the Prophet

وَالتَّقْلِيدُ هُوَ قَبُولُ قَوْلِ قَائِلٍ يَغْلِبُ عَلَى الظَّنِّ صِدْقُهُ لِحُسْنِ الثِّقَةِ فِيهِ. وَالْفَرْقُ بَيْنَ هَذَا وَبَيْنَ تَقْلِيدِهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ، أَنَّ تَقْلِيدَهُ هُوَ قَبُولُ قَوْلٍ يَقَعُ لِلْإِنْسَانِ الْيَقِينُ بِهِ لِدَلَالَةِ الْمُعْجِزَةِ عَلَى صِدْقِهِ صَلَّى اللَّهُ عَلَيْهِ وَسَلَّمَ.

Following authority (تقليد) is accepting someone’s statement whose truth is judged preponderantly probable because of confidence in that person. It differs from following the Prophet, God bless him and grant him peace: following him is accepting a statement of which one attains certainty, because the miracle indicates his truthfulness, God bless him and grant him peace.

§250 · Why juristic expertise is a collective obligation

وَأَمَّا مَنْ يَجُوزُ لَهُمُ التَّقْلِيدُ، فَهُمُ الْعَوَامُّ، بِدَلِيلِ أَنَّ النَّاسَ لَا يَخْلُونَ مِنْ ثَلَاثَةِ أَقْسَامٍ: إِمَّا أَنْ يَكُونُوا كُلُّهُمْ مُجْتَهِدِينَ، وَهَذَا مُحَالٌ وُقُوعُهُ وَالتَّكْلِيفُ بِهِ، لِأَنَّهُ كَانَ يُؤَدِّي إِلَى انْقِطَاعِ الْمَعَايِشِ لَوْ كَانَ مُمْكِنًا أَنْ يَحْصُلَ لِكُلِّ أَحَدٍ رُتْبَةُ الِاجْتِهَادِ.

Those permitted to follow authority are ordinary people. The proof is that people must fall under one of three arrangements. Either all are independent jurists; this is impossible both in practice and as an obligation, since, even if everyone could attain that rank, it would bring the means of livelihood to a halt.

وَإِمَّا أَنْ تُفْقَدَ فِي جَمِيعِهِمْ شُرُوطُ الِاجْتِهَادِ، وَهَذَا أَيْضًا مُمْتَنِعٌ، لِأَنَّهُ كَانَ يُؤَدِّي إِلَى إِهْمَالِ أَكْثَرِ الْأَحْكَامِ، إِذْ أَكْثَرُ الْفَرَائِضِ وَالسُّنَنِ إِنَّمَا يَقُومُ بِفَرْضِ مَعْرِفَتِهَا وَتَعْلِيمِهَا لِلنَّاسِ الْمُجْتَهِدُونَ.

Or all lack the qualifications for independent inquiry. This too is impossible, because it would lead to the neglect of most rulings: independent jurists are those who discharge the obligation of knowing most prescribed duties and practices and teaching them to people.

وَإِمَّا أَنْ يُوجَدَ فِي النَّاسِ الصِّنْفَانِ جَمِيعًا، وَهُوَ أَنْ تَكُونَ فِيهِمْ طَائِفَةٌ تَقُومُ لِلْجُمْهُورِ بِضَبْطِ الْفَرَائِضِ وَالسُّنَنِ، وَجَعْلِهَا عَلَيْهِمْ، وَأَخْذِهِمْ بِهَا، وَاسْتِنْبَاطِ مَا شَأْنُهُ أَنْ يُسْتَنْبَطَ مِنْهَا فِي وَقْتٍ وَقْتٍ وَنَازِلَةٍ نَازِلَةٍ. وَتَكُونَ فِيهِمْ طَائِفَةٌ أُخْرَى، وَهُمُ الْعَوَامُّ، شَأْنُهُمْ تَقْلِيدُ هَؤُلَاءِ لِحُسْنِ الثِّقَةِ بِهِمْ، وَغَلَبَةِ الظَّنِّ فِي صِدْقِهِمْ.

Or both classes exist: one group preserves the prescribed duties and practices for the public, lays them upon people, requires their observance, and derives what may be derived from them at each time and in each case. Another group, ordinary people, follows the authority of the first because of confidence in them and a preponderant judgement of their truthfulness.

وَكَأَنَّ غَلَبَةَ الظَّنِّ لِحُسْنِ الثِّقَةِ جُعِلَتْ هَهُنَا أَمَارَةً لِلُزُومِ الْأَحْكَامِ لَهُمْ، كَمَا جُعِلَتْ غَلَبَةُ الظَّنِّ لِلْمُجْتَهِدِ أَمَارَةً لِلُزُومِ الْحُكْمِ لَهُ. وَبِالْوَاجِبِ مَا سُمِّيَ هَذَا فَرْضَ كِفَايَةٍ، إِذْ يَكْفِي فِي الْقِيَامِ بِهِ الْبَعْضُ عَنِ الْبَعْضِ.

Here it is as though preponderant judgement based on confidence has been made a sign that the rulings bind them, just as the independent jurist’s preponderant judgement is a sign that a ruling binds him. This is rightly called a collective obligation (فرض كفاية), since its performance by some suffices on behalf of others.

§251 · May a mujtahid follow a more knowledgeable jurist?

فَقَدْ تَبَيَّنَ مِنْ هَذَا أَنَّ النَّاسَ صِنْفَانِ: صِنْفٌ فَرْضُهُ التَّقْلِيدُ، وَهُمُ الْعَوَامُّ الَّذِينَ لَمْ يَبْلُغُوا رُتْبَةَ الِاجْتِهَادِ الَّتِي حُدِّدَتْ فِيمَا قَبْلُ. وَصِنْفٌ ثَانٍ وَهُمُ الْمُجْتَهِدُونَ الَّذِينَ كَمُلَتْ لَهُمْ شُرُوطُ الِاجْتِهَادِ.

It is therefore clear that people are of two classes. One is obliged to follow authority: ordinary people who have not attained the rank of independent inquiry defined earlier. The second comprises independent jurists who fully possess its qualifications.

وَأَمَّا هَلْ لِهَذَا الصِّنْفِ الثَّانِي وَهُمُ الْمُجْتَهِدُونَ أَنْ يُقَلِّدَ بَعْضُهُمْ بَعْضًا، فَفِيهِ نَظَرٌ. فَإِنَّ تَقْلِيدَ الْعَوَامِّ شَيْءٌ أَدَّتْ إِلَيْهِ الضَّرُورَةُ، وَوَقَعَ عَلَيْهِ الْإِجْمَاعُ. لَكِنْ يَنْبَغِي أَنْ يُقَالَ: يَجُوزُ لِلْمُجْتَهِدِ تَقْلِيدُ الْمُجْتَهِدِ إِذَا كَانَ أَعْلَمَ مِنْهُ، وَتَرَجَّحَ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الظَّنِّ بِهِ تَرَجُّحًا يَفْضُلُ عِنْدَهُ الظَّنَّ الْوَاقِعَ لَهُ فِي الشَّيْءِ عَنِ اجْتِهَادِهِ.

Whether those in the second class may follow one another’s authority requires examination. Ordinary people’s following authority is required by necessity and established by consensus. Nevertheless, one should say that an independent jurist may follow another who is more knowledgeable than he, when his confidence in the other’s judgement outweighs the probable judgement he himself reaches through independent inquiry into the matter.

§252 · Memorised school doctrine is not an independent source

وَلِأَنَّ هَهُنَا طَائِفَةً تُشْبِهُ الْعَوَامَّ مِنْ جِهَةٍ، وَالْمُجْتَهِدِينَ مِنْ جِهَةٍ، وَهُمُ الْمُسَمَّوْنَ فِي زَمَانِنَا هَذَا أَكْثَرَ ذَلِكَ بِالْفُقَهَاءِ، فَيَنْبَغِي أَنْ نَنْظُرَ فِي أَيِّ الصِّنْفَيْنِ أَوْلَى أَنْ نُلْحِقَهُمْ. وَهُوَ ظَاهِرٌ مِنْ أَمْرِهِمْ أَنَّ مَرْتَبَتَهُمْ مَرْتَبَةُ الْعَوَامِّ، وَأَنَّهُمْ مُقَلِّدُونَ.

There is a group resembling ordinary people in one respect and independent jurists in another: those mostly called jurists in our time. We should examine to which class they more properly belong. Their condition plainly shows that their rank is that of ordinary people and that they are followers of authority.

وَالْفَرْقُ بَيْنَ هَؤُلَاءِ وَبَيْنَ الْعَوَامِّ، أَنَّهُمْ يَحْفَظُونَ الْآرَاءَ الَّتِي لِلْمُجْتَهِدِينَ فَيُخْبِرُونَ عَنْهَا الْعَوَامَّ، مِنْ غَيْرِ أَنْ تَكُونَ عِنْدَهُمْ شُرُوطُ الِاجْتِهَادِ، فَكَأَنَّ مَرْتَبَتَهُمْ فِي ذَلِكَ مَرْتَبَةُ النَّاقِلِينَ عَنِ الْمُجْتَهِدِينَ. وَلَوْ وَقَفُوا فِي هَذَا لَكَانَ الْأَمْرُ أَشْبَهَ، لَكِنْ يَتَعَدَّوْنَ فَيَقِيسُونَ أَشْيَاءَ لَمْ يُنْقَلْ فِيهَا عَنْ مُقَلَّدِيهِمْ حُكْمٌ عَلَى مَا نُقِلَ عَنْهُ فِي ذَلِكَ حُكْمٌ، فَيَجْعَلُونَ أَصْلًا مَا لَيْسَ بِأَصْلٍ، وَيُصَيِّرُونَ أَقَاوِيلَ الْمُجْتَهِدِينَ أُصُولًا لِاجْتِهَادِهِمْ، وَكَفَى بِهَذَا ضَلَالًا وَبِدْعَةً.

They differ from ordinary people by memorising independent jurists’ opinions and reporting them to others, without themselves possessing the qualifications for independent inquiry. Their rank is thus like that of transmitters from independent jurists. Had they stopped there, matters would have been more fitting. Instead, they go beyond it, analogising cases for which no ruling has been transmitted from their authorities to cases for which one has. They make a source of what is not a source, turning independent jurists’ statements into sources for their own inquiry. That is sufficient error and innovation!

§253 · Why inherited opinions cannot replace living inquiry

فَأَمَّا هَلْ يَجُوزُ لَهُمُ الْحَالُ الْأُولَى، وَهِيَ أَنْ يَكُونُوا فِي ذَلِكَ نَاقِلِينَ عَنْ مُجْتَهِدٍ غَلَبَ عَلَى ظَنِّهِمْ إِصَابَتُهُ وَيَتَحَرَّى ذَلِكَ خَلَفُهُمْ عَنْ سَلَفِهِمْ فِي النَّقْلِ حَتَّى يَكُونَ الْقَائِمُونَ بِفَرْضِ الِاجْتِهَادِ غَيْرَ مَوْجُودِينَ فِي زَمَانِنَا هَذَا مَثَلًا، إِلَّا مَا قَدْ سَلَفَ وَيَكْفِيَ فِي ذَلِكَ مَثَلًا أَنْ كَانَ فِي الصَّدْرِ الْأَوَّلِ مَنْ قَامَ بِهَذَا الْفَرْضِ وَلَوْ رَجُلٌ وَاحِدٌ أَوْ أَكْثَرُ مِنْ وَاحِدٍ، عَلَى مِثَالِ مَا أَدْرَكْنَا عَلَيْهِ هَذِهِ الطَّوَائِفَ، أَعْنِي الْمَالِكِيَّةَ وَالشَّافِعِيَّةَ وَالْحَنَفِيَّةَ،

May they, however, remain in the first condition: transmitting from an independent jurist whom they judge probably correct, with successors carefully transmitting from predecessors, so that, for example, no one in our time discharges the obligation of independent inquiry, apart from those of the past? Would it suffice that someone in the earliest period discharged it, even one man or more, as we find among these groups—the Mālikīs, Shāfiʿīs, and Ḥanafīs?

فَيَدُلُّ لَعَمْرِي عَلَى امْتِنَاعِ ذَلِكَ انْعِقَادُ الْإِجْمَاعِ عَلَى أَنَّ جَمِيعَ فُرُوضِ الْكِفَايَاتِ يَنْبَغِي أَنْ يَكُونَ فِي زَمَانٍ زَمَانٍ مَنْ يَقُومُ بِهَا، وَحِينَئِذٍ تَسْقُطُ عَنِ الْغَيْرِ. وَأَيْضًا فَإِنَّ النَّوَازِلَ الْوَاقِعَةَ غَيْرُ مُتَنَاهِيَةٍ، وَلَيْسَ يُمْكِنُ نَقْلُ قَوْلٍ قَوْلٍ عَنْ مَنْ سَلَفَ مِنَ الْمُجْتَهِدِينَ فِي نَازِلَةٍ نَازِلَةٍ، فَإِنَّ ذَلِكَ مُمْتَنِعٌ.

By my life, consensus proves that this is impermissible: for every collective obligation there must be someone in every period who performs it; only then does it cease to bind the others. Moreover, the cases that arise are without limit. It is impossible to transmit from earlier independent jurists a separate pronouncement for every case.

§254 · No period should lack an active mujtahid

وَإِذَا كَانَ ذَلِكَ كَذَلِكَ فَلَمْ يَبْقَ إِلَّا وَاحِدٌ مِنْ ثَلَاثَةٍ: إِمَّا أَنْ نَجْعَلَ أَقَاوِيلَ مَنْ سَلَفَ مِنَ الْمُجْتَهِدِينَ فِيمَا أَفْتَوْا فِيهِ أُصُولًا يُسْتَنْبَطُ عَنْهَا. وَإِمَّا أَنْ يَتَعَطَّلَ كَثِيرٌ مِنَ الْأَحْكَامِ. وَكِلَا الْوَجْهَيْنِ مُمْتَنِعٌ، فَلَمْ يَبْقَ إِلَّا الْوَجْهُ الثَّالِثُ وَهُوَ أَلَّا يَخْلُوَ زَمَانٌ مِنْ مُجْتَهِدٍ.

This leaves only three possibilities: to make earlier independent jurists’ legal opinions into sources from which rulings are derived; to leave many rulings inoperative; or, since both alternatives are impermissible, to accept the third—that no period should be without an independent jurist.[N120: translation] “Practicing” makes explicit the distinction in the Arabic between possessing the rank of ijtihād and actually exercising it. The criticism concerns the author’s own preceding period; it is not a claim that independent juristic inquiry historically ceased in every place. ↩

وَأَنْتَ تَعْلَمُ أَنَّ الزَّمَانَ الَّذِي سَلَفَ هَكَذَا كَانَتْ حَالُهُ، أَعْنِي أَنَّهُ لَمْ يَكُنْ فِيهِ مُجْتَهِدٌ. وَإِنْ كَانَ لَا يُدْفَعُ أَنَّهُ قَدْ كَانَ فِيهِ مَنْ بَلَغَ رُتْبَةَ الِاجْتِهَادِ. لَكِنْ مَعَ هَذَا فَإِنَّمَا كَانَ مُقَلِّدًا.

You know that the period just past was in this condition: it had no practising independent jurist. Although it cannot be denied that someone in it had attained the rank of independent inquiry, he nevertheless acted only as a follower of authority.

§255 · The valid and invalid uses of following authority

فَقَدْ تَبَيَّنَ مِنْ هَذَا الْقَوْلِ مَنِ الْمُقَلِّدُ، وَمَا التَّقْلِيدُ، وَفِي أَيِّ الْمَوَاضِعِ يَصِحُّ وَفِي أَيِّهَا لَا.

This discussion has clarified who follows authority, what following authority is, and where it is and is not valid.

§256 · Why an exhaustive catalogue of preferences is unnecessary

وَبَقِيَ الْقَوْلُ فِي الْفَصْلِ الثَّالِثِ وَهُوَ يَتَضَمَّنُ الْقَوْلَ فِي تَرْجِيحِ طُرُقِ النَّقْلِ، وَتَرْجِيحِ مَا تَدُلُّ عَلَيْهِ الْأَلْفَاظُ بِمَفْهُومَاتِهَا، وَذَلِكَ فِيمَا كَانَ مِنْ ذَلِكَ مِنْ نَوْعٍ وَاحِدٍ، لِأَنَّ الْأَدِلَّةَ الَّتِي أَنْوَاعُهَا مُخْتَلِفَةٌ قَدْ تَبَيَّنَ تَرَجُّحُ بَعْضِهَا عَلَى بَعْضٍ فِيمَا سَلَفَ، وَهِيَ قَوَانِينُ تَقْتَرِنُ بِدَلِيلٍ دَلِيلٍ وَسَنَدٍ وَسَنَدٍ وَتَكَادُ لَا تَتَنَاهَى.

There remains the third chapter, concerning the comparative weight of transmission routes and of what expressions signify through their understood meanings. This concerns evidence of the same kind, since the relative weight of different kinds has already been established. The relevant rules attach to particular items of evidence and particular chains of transmission, and are virtually without limit.

وَقَدْ رَامَ أَهْلُ هَذِهِ الصِّنَاعَةِ حَصْرَهَا، لَكِنْ أَمَّا نَحْنُ فَلَا حَاجَةَ لَنَا إِلَى تَعْدِيدِهَا، إِذْ كَانَ الْإِنْسَانُ يُمْكِنُهُ مِنْ تِلْقَاءِ نَفْسِهِ الْوُقُوفُ عَلَى مَا فِيهَا يُفِيدُ غَلَبَةَ ظَنٍّ مِمَّا لَيْسَ يُفِيدُ. فَأَمَّا مَنْ كَانَ لَهُ فَرَاغٌ، وَأَحَبَّ أَنْ يُثْبِتَهَا هَهُنَا فَلْيَفْعَلْ، لَكِنْ يَجِبُ أَنْ يُثْبِتَ الْقَرَائِنَ الَّتِي يَقَعُ بِهَا تَرْجِيحُ طُرُقِ النَّقْلِ فِي كِتَابِ الْأَخْبَارِ، وَالَّتِي يَقَعُ بِهَا تَرْجِيحُ الْمَفْهُومِ فِي الْجُزْءِ الثَّالِثِ مِنْ هَذَا الْكِتَابِ.

Practitioners of this discipline have attempted to enumerate them. We have no need to do so, since a person can determine for himself which produce a preponderant judgement and which do not. Anyone with leisure who wishes to record them here may do so. However, the contextual indications establishing the superiority of transmission routes should be placed in the discussion of reports, and those establishing the superiority of an understood meaning in Part Three of this book.

§257 · The obligation to follow the stronger judgement

فَأَمَّا أَنَّ الْمَصِيرَ إِلَى الْعَمَلِ بِأَرْجَحِ الظَّنَّيْنِ دَلِيلٌ شَرْعِيٌّ، فَيَدُلُّ عَلَيْهِ إِجْمَاعُ الصَّحَابَةِ عَلَى ذَلِكَ، وَمَا يَجِدُهُ الْإِنْسَانُ فِي نَفْسِهِ مِنَ الْمَصِيرِ إِلَى أَرْجَحِ الظَّنَّيْنِ عِنْدَ تَعَارُضِهِمَا.

That adopting and acting upon the stronger of two probable judgements constitutes legal evidence is shown by the Companions’ consensus on this, and by what a person finds in himself: he turns to the stronger of two probable judgements when they conflict.

§258 · An abridgement completed and supplemented: 552 AH

وَهُنَا انْتَهَى غَرَضُنَا فِي هَذَا الِاخْتِصَارِ. وَهُوَ يُشْبِهُ الْمُخْتَصَرَ مِنْ جِهَةِ حَذْفِ التَّطْوِيلِ، وَالْمُخْتَرَعَ مِنْ جِهَةِ التَّتْمِيمِ وَالتَّكْمِيلِ. وَذَلِكَ فِي الْعَشْرِ الْوُسْطِ مِنْ ذِي الْحِجَّةِ مِنْ سَنَةِ اثْنَتَيْنِ وَخَمْسِينَ وَخَمْسِ مِائَةٍ.

Here our purpose in this abridgement is complete. It resembles an abridgement in removing prolixity, and an original work in its additions and completions. This was during the middle ten days of Dhū al-Ḥijja in the year five hundred and fifty-two.[N121: translation] This is Ibn Rushd’s authorial conclusion and date, 552 AH. The following 606 AH copying notice and ownership statement belong to later manuscript voices and are presented separately. ↩[N122: lexical] الْوُسْط is retained with ḍamma and sukūn. This is an attested reading of the calendrical expression العشر الوسط: Ibn Ḥajar, Fatḥ al-bārī on the report about the middle ten nights (Bukhārī 2016), records al-Bājī’s sukūn reading alongside other readings. No change to the date is intended. https://hadithprophet.com/hadith-32830.html ↩

Glossary · Arabic and English

adāʾ — Timely performance: discharging an act within its legally appointed time; distinguish repetition and make-up performance.

ʿadāla — Probity: a disposition supporting a transmitter’s reliability, not a claim that every lapse destroys credibility.

ʿāmm — General expression: wording embracing the instances of a meaning. Do not confuse this linguistic scope with several equivocal meanings.

ʿaql — Intellect or reason; its relation to goodness, obligation and revealed authority is discussed without making theology the whole of uṣūl.

ʿazīma / ʿazm — Original legal requirement, contrasted with a concession. The text uses the vocabulary of the unrelaxed requirement in defining the contrast.

aṣl — Foundation, source, original case, or governing principle. In qiyās it is the case or meaning from which extension proceeds.

bāṭil / buṭlān — Invalid / invalidity. In contracts, absence of the intended legal effect; invalidity is not interchangeable with moral prohibition.

bayān — Clarification: whatever validly brings a meaning to understanding, including wording, context and explanatory action.

barāʾa aṣliyya — Original freedom from liability: obligation requires a warrant; absence of such a warrant is not itself a newly revealed duty.

burhān — Demonstration or conclusive proof. Its logical analysis belongs to a different discipline from the present abridgement’s immediate task.

ḍamīr — Pronoun: an expression whose referent can be determinate or ambiguous; reference is one component of verbal indication.

ḍarūrī — Necessary; in knowledge, immediately or inevitably known rather than acquired through a consciously constructed argument. In the title, what is indispensable to the craft.

ḍarūra — Necessity or constraint. In practical law it can ground a concession; this is distinct from the epistemic sense of necessary knowledge.

ḍidd — Contrary: opposed acts or qualities. Whether alternatives exhaust the possibilities is crucial to the argument about commands and prohibitions.

dalāla — Indication or signification: the relation by which words, understood meanings, acts or approval make a ruling intelligible.

dalīl — Evidence, indicator or proof. Its strength may be conclusive or probable; “proof” does not always mean logical demonstration.

dalīl al-khiṭāb — Contrary implication inferred from a restriction, condition or qualification. Its force depends on context, not form alone.

farʿ — Derivative case or branch of law; in qiyās, the case whose ruling is sought through a relation to the original.

farḍ — Obligatory requirement. The discussion records a distinction from wājib made by some jurists and its evidential basis.

fasād — Corruption or invalidity; in legal use, failure of an act or contract in the respect relevant to its ruling.

fiqh — Understanding of practical legal rulings. Detailed branch-law learning is related to, but not identical with, the requirements of independent inquiry.

fiʿl — Act: voluntary human performance or Prophetic action. An act has no imperative grammatical form of its own.

furūʿ — Branches or derivative rulings, distinguished from sources and the general method for understanding their indications.

ghāya — End or limit. In a temporal expression such as “until night,” it means an endpoint, not a purposive final cause.

ghalabat al-ẓann — Preponderant judgement: the balance of probable evidence which guides action; it does not create truth merely through preference.

ḥadd — Definition or boundary. In another context, a prescribed penalty; these senses must not be collapsed into one English rendering.

Correspondences in the Bidāya

ApplicationBookExplanatory note
Marriage: one form of act, different legal rulingsMarriageThe majority recommend marriage; the Ẓāhirīs require it; later Mālikīs distinguish obligatory, recommended and permitted marriage according to the person’s circumstances. Ibn Rushd identifies both the interpretation of imperative expressions and an appeal to welfare as causes of the disagreement. This illustrates the distinction between an act’s general description and the ruling applicable to a situated agent. It does not prove that an imperative by itself encodes all those contextual differences. The welfare argument reported here should also be compared with the stricter controls of Darūrī
Wiping the head: a minimum performance and a contested particlePurification from ritual impurityMālik requires the whole head, al-Shāfiʿī allows part, and Abū Ḥanīfa specifies a quarter. Ibn Rushd relates the disagreement to the bāʾ in Q 5:6—whether emphatic or partitive—and to reports describing the Prophet’s wiping. Even on the emphatic reading, the amount satisfying the name of the act remains disputed. This is a representative application of the Darūrī’s minimum-sufficiency problem, but one in which grammar and transmitted practice jointly determine the legally adequate minimum.
Proposing over another proposal: prohibition and contractual effectMarriageJurists distinguish the prohibition of intervening in an existing proposal from the validity of the ensuing marriage. Ibn Rushd records invalidation by Dāwūd, validity according to al-Shāfiʿī and Abū Ḥanīfa, and differing Mālikī outcomes. The question is whether the forbidden preliminary act vitiates the contract or is an independently culpable circumstance. Read this alongside the Darūrī’s distinctions of aspect and the later question whether prohibition entails invalidity; the note identifies a legal parallel, not an explicit cross-citation between the books.
Zakāt on a child’s property: worship or a claim upon wealth?ZakātMālik, al-Shāfiʿī and others impose zakāt on a minor’s property; other views exempt it or distinguish agricultural produce. Ibn Rushd identifies the underlying question: is zakāt principally worship requiring adult responsibility, or a financial right of the poor in wealth? The latter need not depend on the owner’s puberty. This demonstrates why exemption from direct moral obligation does not automatically remove every legal consequence affecting a child’s property.
